O\left(\frac{(S^{3}A)^{\frac{1}{4}}\sqrt{T}}{(1-\gamma)^{\frac{7}{2}}\alpha^{(T)}}\right)\;=\;O\left(\frac{(S^{3}A)^{\frac{1}{4}}T^{\frac{5}{6}}}{(1-\gamma)^{\frac{7}{2}}}\right).

Markov competitive game
.
We start from an intermediate step in the proof of Theorem 1 of
(
Wei et al. 2021b
)
. Specifically, they have shown that if both players use
Algorithm
3
in a two-player zero-sum Markov game, then,

\displaystyle\sum_{t\,=\,1}^{T}\left(\max_{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}V^{x^{\prime},y^{(t)}}(\rho)-V^{x^{(t)},y^{\prime}}(\rho)\right)\;=\;O\left(\frac{S\sqrt{C_{\alpha}C_{\beta}T}}{\eta(1-\gamma)}\right)

where
C_{\alpha}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=1+\sum_{t\,=\,1}^{T}\alpha^{(t)}
and
C_{\beta}
is an upper bound for
\sum_{t\,=\,\tau}^{T}\beta^{(t,\tau)}
with
\beta^{(t,\tau)}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\alpha^{(\tau)}\prod_{i\,=\,\tau}^{t-1}(1-\alpha^{(i)}+\alpha^{(i)}\gamma)
if
\tau<t
and
\beta^{(t,t)}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=1
. We next calculate the upper bounds for
C_{\alpha}
and
C_{\beta}
.

Bounding
C_{\alpha}
.
Recall that
\alpha^{(t)}=\frac{1}{6}t^{-\frac{1}{3}}
. By the definition of
C_{\alpha}
,

C_{\alpha}\;=\;1\,+\,\frac{1}{6}\sum_{t\,=\,1}^{T}t^{-\frac{1}{3}}\;=\;O\left(T^{\frac{2}{3}}\right).

Bounding
C_{\beta}
.
Using
\alpha^{(t)}=\frac{1}{6}t^{-\frac{1}{3}}
, for any
\tau\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t\,=\,\tau}^{T}\beta^{(t,\tau)}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;1\,+\,\sum_{t\,=\,\tau+1}^{T}\alpha^{(\tau)}\prod_{i\,=\,\tau}^{t-1}(1-\alpha^{(i)}+\alpha^{(i)}\gamma)

\displaystyle\;=\;1\,+\,\frac{1}{6}\sum_{t\,=\,\tau+1}^{T}\tau^{-\frac{1}{3}}\left(1-\frac{1}{6}t^{-\frac{1}{3}}(1-\gamma)\right)^{t-\tau}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;1\,+\,\frac{1}{6}\sum_{t\,=\,\tau+1}^{t_{0}}\tau^{-\frac{1}{3}}+\frac{1}{6}\sum_{t\,=\,t_{0}+1}^{T}\tau^{-\frac{1}{3}}\left(1-\frac{1}{6}t^{-\frac{1}{3}}(1-\gamma)\right)^{t-\tau}

(for some
t_{0}
defined below)

\displaystyle\;\leq\;1\,+\,\frac{1}{6}\tau^{-\frac{1}{3}}(t_{0}-\tau)\,+\,\frac{1}{6}\tau^{-\frac{1}{3}}\sum_{t\,=\,t_{0}+1}^{T}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{6}t^{-\frac{1}{3}}(1-\gamma)(t-\tau)\right).

(35)

Define
t_{0}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\tau+H(\tau+c)^{\frac{1}{3}}\ln(\tau+c)+c
, where
H:=\frac{48}{1-\gamma}
and
c:=2\left(\frac{2H}{1-\frac{1}{3}}\ln\frac{H}{1-\frac{1}{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-\frac{1}{3}}}
(if
t_{0}>T
, we simply ignore the second term in
(
35
)
). By
Lemma
16
with
q=\frac{1}{3}
, for all
t\geq t_{0}
,

\displaystyle t-\tau\;\geq\;\frac{H}{2}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\ln\left(\frac{t}{2}\right).

Hence, we can continue to bound the right-hand side of
(
35
)
by

\displaystyle O\left(H\left(\frac{\tau+c}{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\ln(\tau+c)+\frac{c}{\tau^{\frac{1}{3}}}\right)\,+\,\frac{1}{6}\tau^{-\frac{1}{3}}\sum_{t\,=\,t_{0}+1}^{T}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{12}t^{-\frac{1}{3}}(1-\gamma)\frac{H}{2}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\ln\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\;\widetilde{O}\big(H(1+c)^{\frac{1}{3}}+c\big)\,+\,\frac{1}{6}\tau^{-\frac{1}{3}}\sum_{t\,=\,t_{0}+1}^{T}\frac{2}{t}

\displaystyle=\;\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)

which proves that
C_{\beta}=\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)
.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{t\,=\,1}^{T}\left(\max_{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}V^{x^{\prime},y^{(t)}}(\rho)-V^{x^{(t)},y^{\prime}}(\rho)\right)

\displaystyle\;=\;O\left(\frac{S\sqrt{C_{\alpha}C_{\beta}T}}{\eta(1-\gamma)}\right)\;=\;\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{ST^{\frac{5}{6}}}{\eta(1-\gamma)^{\frac{7}{4}}}\right)

which completes the proof by taking
\eta=\frac{(1-\gamma)^{2}}{32\sqrt{SA}}
.
∎

Lemma 16
.

Fix
\tau\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<q<1
,
H\geq 1
. Let

\displaystyle t_{0}\;\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\;\tau\,+\,H(\tau+c)^{q}\ln(\tau+c)\,+\,c

where
c\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=2\left(\frac{2H}{1-q}\ln\frac{H}{1-q}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-q}}
.
Then for all
t\geq t_{0}
,

\displaystyle t\,-\,H\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)^{q}\ln\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)\;\geq\;\tau.

Proof of
Lemma
16
.

We first show that for all
t\geq c
,

•

Ht^{q}\ln t\leq t
;

•

t-H\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)^{q}\ln\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)
is non-decreasing.

To show the two items above, we apply Lemma A.1 of
(
Shalev-Shwartz & Ben-David 2014
)
which states that
x\geq 2a\ln(a)\Rightarrow x\geq a\ln(x)
for any
a>0
. By the definition of
c
, for all
t\geq c
,
t^{1-q}\geq\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)^{1-q}\geq\frac{2H}{1-q}\ln\frac{H}{1-q}
and thus

\displaystyle t^{1-q}

\displaystyle\;\geq\;\frac{H}{1-q}\ln(t^{1-q})\;=\;H\ln t

which proves the first item, and that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)^{1-q}\;\geq\;\frac{H}{1-q}\ln\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)^{1-q}\;=\;H\ln\frac{t}{2}

which gives

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(t-\frac{H}{2}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)^{q}\ln\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\;=\;1\,-\,\frac{H}{2}\cdot\frac{q}{2}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)^{q-1}\ln\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)\,-\,\frac{H}{2}\cdot\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)^{q}\frac{1}{t}

\displaystyle\;\geq\;1\,-\,\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{q}{2}\,-\,\frac{1}{2}\;\geq\;0

which proves the second item.

By the first item and the definition of
t_{0}
,
t_{0}\leq\tau+(\tau+c)+c=2\tau+2c
. Then by the second item, for all
t\geq t_{0}
we have

\displaystyle t\,-\,\frac{H}{2}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)^{q}\ln\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)\;\geq\;t_{0}\,-\,\frac{H}{2}\left(\frac{t_{0}}{2}\right)^{q}\ln\left(\frac{t_{0}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\;\geq\;t_{0}\,-\,\frac{H}{2}\left(\tau+c\right)^{q}\ln\left(\tau+c\right)\;\geq\;\tau

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 17
.

For any two policies
(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})
and
(x,y)
,

\max_{s}\left\|{Q_{s}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}-Q_{s}^{x,y}}\right\|_{\max}\;\leq\;\frac{\gamma}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\max_{s^{\prime}}\left(\left\|{x_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}-x_{s^{\prime}}}\right\|_{1}+\left\|{y_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}-y_{s^{\prime}}}\right\|_{1}\right).

Proof of
Lemma
17
.

By the definition,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}&&\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\left\|{Q_{s}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}-Q_{s}^{x,y}}\right\|_{\max}\\[5.69046pt]
&\overset{(a)}{\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=}&\displaystyle\max_{a_{1},a_{2}}\left|Q_{s}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(a_{1},a_{2})-Q_{s}^{x,y}(a_{1},a_{2})\right|\\[5.69046pt]
&\overset{(b)}{\leq}&\displaystyle\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,\bar{a}_{1},\bar{a}_{2})\left|(x_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime})^{\top}Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}y_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}-(x_{s^{\prime}})^{\top}Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x,y}y_{s^{\prime}}\right|\\[5.69046pt]
&\leq&\displaystyle\gamma\max_{s^{\prime}}\underbrace{\left|(x_{s^{\prime}})^{\top}Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}y_{s^{\prime}}-(x_{s^{\prime}})^{\top}Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x,y}y_{s^{\prime}}\right|}_{\textbf{Qiff}}\end{array}

(36)

where
\bar{a}_{1}
and
\bar{a}_{2}
achieve the maximum in
(a)
, and
(b)
is due to the Bellman equation,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}Q_{s}^{x,y}(a_{1},a_{2})&=&\displaystyle r(s,a_{1},a_{2})\,+\,\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\,\sim\,\mathbb{P}(\cdot\,|\,s,a_{1},a_{2})}\left[V_{s^{\prime}}^{x,y}\right]\\[5.69046pt]
&=&\displaystyle r(s,a_{1},a_{2})\,+\,\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a_{1},a_{2})\sum_{a_{1}^{\prime},a_{2}^{\prime}}x_{s^{\prime}}(a_{1}^{\prime})y_{s^{\prime}}(a_{2}^{\prime})Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x,y}(a_{1}^{\prime},a_{2}^{\prime}).\end{array}

Fix
s^{\prime}
, we next subtract and add
(x_{s^{\prime}})^{\top}Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}y_{s^{\prime}}
in
Qiff
and apply
|a+b|\leq|a|+|b|
to reach,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\textbf{Qiff}&\leq&\displaystyle\left|(x_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime})^{\top}Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}y_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}-(x_{s^{\prime}})^{\top}Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}y_{s^{\prime}}\right|\,+\,\left|(x_{s^{\prime}})^{\top}Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}y_{s^{\prime}}-(x_{s^{\prime}})^{\top}Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x,y}y_{s^{\prime}}\right|\\[5.69046pt]
&\leq&\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\left|\sum_{a_{1}^{\prime},a_{2}^{\prime}}\left(x_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}(a_{1})y_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}(a_{2}^{\prime})-x_{s^{\prime}}(a_{1}^{\prime})y_{s^{\prime}}(a_{2}^{\prime})\right)Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(a_{1}^{\prime},a_{2}^{\prime})\right|\\
&&\displaystyle+\,\left|(x_{s^{\prime}})^{\top}\left(Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}-Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x,y}\right)y_{s^{\prime}}\right|\\[5.69046pt]
&\leq&\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\sum_{a_{1}^{\prime},a_{2}^{\prime}}\left|x_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}(a_{1}^{\prime})y_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}(a_{2}^{\prime})-x_{s^{\prime}}(a_{1}^{\prime})y_{s^{\prime}}(a_{2}^{\prime})\right|\,+\,\left|(x_{s^{\prime}})^{\top}\left(Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}-Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x,y}\right)y_{s^{\prime}}\right|.\end{array}

We also notice that

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\left\|{x_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}\circ y_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}-x_{s^{\prime}}\circ y_{s^{\prime}}}\right\|_{1}&\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=&\displaystyle\sum_{a_{1}^{\prime},a_{2}^{\prime}}\left|x_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}(a_{1}^{\prime})y_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}(a_{2}^{\prime})-x_{s^{\prime}}(a_{1}^{\prime})y_{s^{\prime}}(a_{2}^{\prime})\right|\\[5.69046pt]
&\leq&\displaystyle\sum_{a_{1}^{\prime},a_{2}^{\prime}}\left|x_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}(a_{1}^{\prime})y_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}(a_{2}^{\prime})-x_{s^{\prime}}(a_{1}^{\prime})y_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}(a_{2}^{\prime})\right|\\[5.69046pt]
&&\displaystyle+\,\sum_{a_{1}^{\prime},a_{2}^{\prime}}\left|x_{s^{\prime}}(a_{1}^{\prime})y_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}(a_{2}^{\prime})-x_{s^{\prime}}(a_{1}^{\prime})y_{s^{\prime}}(a_{2}^{\prime})\right|\\[5.69046pt]
&\leq&\displaystyle\sum_{a_{1}^{\prime}}\left|x_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}(a_{1}^{\prime})-x_{s^{\prime}}(a_{1}^{\prime})\right|\,+\,\sum_{a_{2}^{\prime}}\left|y_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}(a_{2}^{\prime})-y_{s^{\prime}}(a_{2}^{\prime})\right|\\[5.69046pt]
&=&\left\|{x_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}-x_{s^{\prime}}}\right\|_{1}\,+\,\left\|{y_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}-y_{s^{\prime}}}\right\|_{1}\end{array}

and

\left|(x_{s^{\prime}})^{\top}\left(Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}-Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x,y}\right)y_{s^{\prime}}\right|\;\leq\;\max_{a_{1},a_{2}}\left|Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(a_{1},a_{2})-Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x,y}(a_{1},a_{2})\right|\;\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\;\left\|{Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}-Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x,y}}\right\|_{\max}.

By substituting the upper bound on
Qiff
above into
(
36
)
,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}&&\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\left\|{Q_{s}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}-Q_{s}^{x,y}}\right\|_{\max}\\[5.69046pt]
&\leq&\displaystyle\gamma\max_{s^{\prime}}\dfrac{1}{1-\gamma}\left(\left\|{x_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}-x_{s^{\prime}}}\right\|_{1}+\left\|{y_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}-y_{s^{\prime}}}\right\|_{1}\right)\,+\,\gamma\max_{s^{\prime}}\left\|{Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}-Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x,y}}\right\|_{\max}.\end{array}

Therefore,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}&&\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\displaystyle\max_{s}\left\|{Q_{s}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}-Q_{s}^{x,y}}\right\|_{\max}\\[5.69046pt]
&\leq&\displaystyle\gamma\max_{s^{\prime}}\dfrac{1}{1-\gamma}\left(\left\|{x_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}-x_{s^{\prime}}}\right\|_{1}+\left\|{y_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}-y_{s^{\prime}}}\right\|_{1}\right)\,+\,\gamma\max_{s^{\prime}}\left\|{Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}-Q_{s^{\prime}}^{x,y}}\right\|_{\max}.\end{array}

which yields the desired result.
∎

Appendix E
Auxiliary Lemmas

In this section, we provide some auxiliary lemmas that are helpful in our analysis.

E.1
Auxiliary lemmas for potential functions

Lemma 18
.

For any
N
-player Markov potential game with instantaneous reward bounded in
[0,1]
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left|\Phi^{\pi}(\mu)-\Phi^{\pi^{\prime}}(\mu)\right|\;\leq\;\frac{N}{1-\gamma}

for any
\pi,\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi
and
\mu\in\Delta({\mathcal{S}})
.

Proof of
Lemma
18
.

By the potential property,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\Phi^{\pi}(\mu)\,-\,\Phi^{\pi^{\prime}}(\mu)&=&(\Phi^{\pi}-\Phi^{\pi_{1}^{\prime},\pi_{-1}})\,+\,(\Phi^{\pi_{1}^{\prime},\pi_{-1}}-\Phi^{\pi_{\{1,2\}}^{\prime},\pi_{-{\{1,2\}}}})\,+\,\ldots\,+\,(\Phi^{\pi_{N},\pi_{-N}^{\prime}}-\Phi^{\pi^{\prime}})\\
&=&(V_{1}^{\pi}-V_{1}^{\pi_{1}^{\prime},\pi_{-1}})\,+\,(V_{2}^{\pi_{1}^{\prime},\pi_{-1}}-V_{2}^{\pi_{\{1,2\}}^{\prime},\pi_{-{\{1,2\}}}})\,+\,\ldots\,+\,(V_{N}^{\pi_{N},\pi_{-N}^{\prime}}-V_{N}^{\pi^{\prime}})\\
&\leq&\displaystyle\frac{N}{1-\gamma}\end{array}

where the last inequality is due to
V_{i}^{\pi}-V_{i}^{\pi^{\prime}}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
for any
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
. By symmetry,
\Phi^{\pi^{\prime}}(\mu)-\Phi^{\pi}(\mu)\leq\frac{N}{1-\gamma}
.
∎

E.2
Auxiliary lemmas for single-player MDPs

We provide some auxiliary lemmas in the context of single-player MDPs.

Lemma 19

(Action value function difference)
.

Suppose that two MDPs have the same state/action spaces, but different reward and transition functions:
(r,p)
and
(\widetilde{r},\widetilde{p})
. Then, for a given policy
\pi
, two action value functions associated with two MDPs satisfy

\displaystyle\max_{s,\,a}|Q^{\pi}(s,a)-\widetilde{Q}^{\pi}(s,a)|\;\leq\;\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\max_{s,\,a}|r(s,a)-\widetilde{r}(s,a)|\,+\,\frac{\gamma}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\max_{s,\,a}\left\|p(\cdot\,|\,s,a)-\widetilde{p}(\cdot\,|\,s,a)\right\|_{1}.

Proof of
Lemma
19
.

By the Bellman equations,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}Q^{\pi}(s,a)&=&\displaystyle r(s,a)\,+\,\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime},\,a^{\prime}}p(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)\,\pi(a^{\prime}\,|\,s^{\prime})\,Q^{\pi}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})\\
\widetilde{Q}^{\pi}(s,a)&=&\displaystyle\widetilde{r}(s,a)\,+\,\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\widetilde{p}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)\,\pi(a^{\prime}\,|\,s^{\prime})\,\widetilde{Q}^{\pi}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime}).\end{array}

Subtracting equalities above on both sides yields

\begin{array}[]{rcl}&&\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!|Q^{\pi}(s,a)-\widetilde{Q}^{\pi}(s,a)|\\[5.69046pt]
&\leq&\displaystyle|r(s,a)-\widetilde{r}(s,a)|\,+\,\gamma\left|\sum_{s^{\prime},\,a^{\prime}}\left(p(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)-\widetilde{p}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)\right)\pi(a^{\prime}\,|\,s^{\prime})Q^{\pi}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})\right|\\[5.69046pt]
&&\displaystyle+\,\gamma\left|\sum_{s^{\prime},\,a^{\prime}}\widetilde{p}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)\pi(a^{\prime}\,|\,s^{\prime})\left(Q^{\pi}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})-\widetilde{Q}^{\pi}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})\right)\right|\\[5.69046pt]
&\leq&\displaystyle|r(s,a)-\widetilde{r}(s,a)|\,+\,\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\left\|p(\cdot\,|\,s,a)-\widetilde{p}(\cdot\,|\,s,a)\right\|_{1}\,+\,\gamma\max_{s^{\prime},\,a^{\prime}}\left|Q^{\pi}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})-\widetilde{Q}^{\pi}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})\right|.\end{array}

Taking the maximum over
(s,a)
leads to

\begin{array}[]{rcl}&&\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\displaystyle\max_{s,\,a}|Q^{\pi}(s,a)-\widetilde{Q}^{\pi}(s,a)|\\[5.69046pt]
&\leq&\displaystyle\max_{s,\,a}|r(s,a)-\widetilde{r}(s,a)|\,+\,\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\max_{s,\,a}\left\|{p(\cdot\,|\,s,a)-\widetilde{p}(\cdot\,|\,s,a)}\right\|_{1}+\gamma\max_{s,\,a}|Q^{\pi}(s,a)-\widetilde{Q}^{\pi}(s,a)|\end{array}

which leads to the desired inequality after rearrangement.
∎

Lemma 20

(Visitation measure difference)
.

Let
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
be two policies for a MDP, and
\mu
be an initial state distribution. Then,

\sum_{s}\left|d^{\pi}_{\mu}(s)-d^{\pi^{\prime}}_{\mu}(s)\right|\;\leq\;\max_{s}\left\|{\pi(\cdot\,|\,s)-\pi^{\prime}(\cdot\,|\,s)}\right\|_{1}.

Proof of
Lemma
20
.

By the definition, for a fixed state
s^{\sharp}
,

d^{\pi}_{\mu}(s^{\sharp})\;=\;(1-\gamma)\mathbb{E}\left[\,\sum_{t\,=\,0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\boldsymbol{1}_{\{s_{t}\,=\,s^{\sharp}\}}~\Big|~s_{0}\sim\mu,~\pi\,\right].

By taking reward function
r(s,a)=(1-\gamma)\boldsymbol{1}_{\{s\,=\,s^{\sharp}\}}
, we
can view
d^{\pi}_{\mu}(s^{\sharp})
as a value function under the policy
\pi
and the initial distribution
\mu
. With a slight abuse of notation, we denote such a value function by
V^{\pi}(\mu;s^{\sharp})=d^{\pi}_{\mu}(s^{\sharp})
. Similarly, we can define
V^{\pi}(s;s^{\sharp})
and
Q^{\pi}(s,a;s^{\sharp})
, using the same reward function.

By the performance difference lemma (a single-player version of
Lemma
1
),

d^{\pi}_{\mu}(s^{\sharp})-d^{\pi^{\prime}}_{\mu}(s^{\sharp})\;=\;V^{\pi}(\mu;s^{\sharp})-V^{\pi^{\prime}}(\mu;s^{\sharp})\;=\;\sum_{s,\,a}d_{\mu}^{\pi}(s)\left(\pi(a\,|\,s)-\pi^{\prime}(a\,|\,s)\right)Q^{\pi^{\prime}}(s,a;s^{\sharp}).

Therefore,

\sum_{s^{\sharp}}\left|d^{\pi}_{\mu}(s^{\sharp})-d^{\pi^{\prime}}_{\mu}(s^{\sharp})\right|\;\leq\;\sum_{s^{\sharp}}\sum_{s,a}d^{\pi}_{\mu}(s)\left|\pi(a\,|\,s)-\pi^{\prime}(a\,|\,s)\right|Q^{\pi^{\prime}}(s,a;s^{\sharp}).

(37)

We also note that
Q^{\pi^{\prime}}(\cdot,\cdot;s^{\sharp})
is the action value function associated with the reward function
r(s,a)=(1-\gamma)\boldsymbol{1}_{\{s\,=\,s^{\sharp}\}}
. Thus,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\displaystyle\sum_{s^{\sharp}}Q^{\pi^{\prime}}(s,a;s^{\sharp})&=&\displaystyle\sum_{s^{\sharp}}\mathbb{E}\left[(1-\gamma)\sum_{t\,=\,0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\boldsymbol{1}_{\{s_{t}\,=\,s^{\sharp}\}}~\Big|~(s_{0},a_{0})=(s,a),~\pi^{\prime}\right]\\[5.69046pt]
&=&\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(1-\gamma)\sum_{t\,=\,0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}~\Big|~(s_{0},a_{0})=(s,a),~\pi^{\prime}\right]\\[5.69046pt]
&=&1.\end{array}

Therefore, we can arrange
(
37
)
as follows,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\displaystyle\sum_{s^{\sharp}}\left|d^{\pi}_{\mu}(s^{\sharp})-d^{\pi^{\prime}}_{\mu}(s^{\sharp})\right|&\leq&\displaystyle\sum_{s,\,a}d^{\pi}_{\mu}(s)\left|\pi(a\,|\,s)-\pi^{\prime}(a\,|\,s)\right|\\[5.69046pt]
&=&\displaystyle\sum_{s}d^{\pi}_{\mu}(s)\left\|{\pi(\cdot\,|\,s)-\pi^{\prime}(\cdot\,|\,s)}\right\|_{1}\\[5.69046pt]
&\leq&\displaystyle\max_{s}\left\|{\pi(\cdot\,|\,s)-\pi^{\prime}(\cdot\,|\,s)}\right\|_{1}.\end{array}

∎

E.3
Auxiliary lemmas for multi-player MDPs

We first extend
Lemma
1
in the 1st-order form to the 2nd-order performance difference, which is useful to measure the joint policy improvement from multiple players.

Lemma 21

(The 2nd-order performance difference)
.

Consider a two-player common-payoff Markov game with state space
{\mathcal{S}}
and action sets
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
. Let
r:{\mathcal{S}}\times{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\rightarrow[0,1]
be the reward function, and
p:{\mathcal{S}}\times{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{A}}_{2}\rightarrow\Delta({\mathcal{S}})
be the transition function. Let
\Pi_{1}=(\Delta({\mathcal{A}}_{1}))^{{\mathcal{S}}}
and
\Pi_{2}=(\Delta({\mathcal{A}}_{2}))^{{\mathcal{S}}}
be player 1 and player 2’s policy sets, respectively. Then, for any
x,x^{\prime}\in\Pi_{1}
and
y,y^{\prime}\in\Pi_{2}
,

\displaystyle V^{x,y}(\mu)\,-\,V^{x^{\prime},y}(\mu)\,-\,V^{x,y^{\prime}}(\mu)\,+\,V^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(\mu)

\displaystyle\leq\;\frac{2\kappa_{\mu}^{2}A}{(1-\gamma)^{4}}\sum_{s}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s)\left(\left\|{x(\cdot\,|\,s)-x^{\prime}(\cdot\,|\,s)}\right\|^{2}+\left\|{y(\cdot\,|\,s)-y^{\prime}(\cdot\,|\,s)}\right\|^{2}\right)

where
\kappa_{\mu}
is the distribution mismatch coefficient relative to
\mu
(see
\kappa_{\mu}
in
Definition
1
).

Proof of
Lemma
21
.

We define the following non-stationary policies:

\displaystyle\bar{x}_{i}:~\text{a Player 1's policy where in steps from $0$ to $i-1$, $x^{\prime}$ is executed; in steps from $i$ to $\infty$, $x$ is executed. }

With this definition,
\bar{x}_{0}=x
and
\bar{x}_{\infty}=x^{\prime}
. We define
\bar{y}_{i}
similarly. Since
\bar{x}_{i}
is non-stationary, we specify its action distribution as
\bar{x}_{i}(\cdot~|~s,h)
where
h
is the step index. The joint value function for these non-stationary policies can be defined as usual:

\displaystyle V^{\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j}}(\mu)\;\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\;\mathbb{E}\left[\,\sum_{t\,=\,0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}r(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})~\bigg|~s_{0}\sim\mu,\;a_{t}\sim\bar{x}_{i}(\cdot\,|\,s_{t},t),\;b_{t}\sim\bar{y}_{j}(\cdot\,|\,s_{t},t)\,\right].

For simplicity, we omit the initial distribution
\mu
in writing the value function.
We first show that for any
H\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle V^{\bar{x}_{0},\bar{y}_{0}}\,-\,V^{\bar{x}_{H},\bar{y}_{0}}\,-\,V^{\bar{x}_{0},\bar{y}_{H}}\,+\,V^{\bar{x}_{H},\bar{y}_{H}}\;=\;\sum_{i\,=\,0}^{H-1}\sum_{j\,=\,0}^{H-1}\left(V^{\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j}}\,-\,V^{\bar{x}_{i+1},\bar{y}_{j}}\,-\,V^{\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j+1}}\,+\,V^{\bar{x}_{i+1},\bar{y}_{j+1}}\right).

In fact, the right-hand side above is equal to

\displaystyle\sum_{j\,=\,0}^{H-1}\sum_{i\,=\,0}^{H-1}\left(V^{\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j}}-V^{\bar{x}_{i+1},\bar{y}_{j}}\right)\,+\,\sum_{j\,=\,0}^{H-1}\sum_{i\,=\,0}^{H-1}\left(-V^{\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j+1}}+V^{\bar{x}_{i+1},\bar{y}_{j+1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\;\sum_{j\,=\,0}^{H-1}\left(V^{\bar{x}_{0},\bar{y}_{j}}-V^{\bar{x}_{H},\bar{y}_{j}}\right)\,+\,\sum_{j\,=\,0}^{H-1}\left(-V^{\bar{x}_{0},\bar{y}_{j+1}}+V^{\bar{x}_{H},\bar{y}_{j+1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\;\sum_{j\,=\,0}^{H-1}\left(V^{\bar{x}_{0},\bar{y}_{j}}-V^{\bar{x}_{0},\bar{y}_{j+1}}\right)\,+\,\sum_{j\,=\,0}^{H-1}\left(-V^{\bar{x}_{H},\bar{y}_{j}}+V^{\bar{x}_{H},\bar{y}_{j+1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\;V^{\bar{x}_{0},\bar{y}_{0}}\,-\,V^{\bar{x}_{0},\bar{y}_{H}}\,-\,V^{\bar{x}_{H},\bar{y}_{0}}\,+\,V^{\bar{x}_{H},\bar{y}_{H}}.

Sending
H
to infinity and recalling that
\bar{x}_{0}=x
,
\bar{x}_{\infty}=x^{\prime}
,
\bar{y}_{0}=y
,
\bar{y}_{\infty}=y^{\prime}
lead to

\displaystyle V^{x,y}\,-\,V^{x^{\prime},y}\,-\,V^{x,y^{\prime}}\,+\,V^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}\;=\;\sum_{i\,=\,0}^{\infty}\sum_{j\,=\,0}^{\infty}\left(V^{\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j}}\,-\,V^{\bar{x}_{i+1},\bar{y}_{j}}\,-\,V^{\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j+1}}\,+\,V^{\bar{x}_{i+1},\bar{y}_{j+1}}\right).

We next focus on the particular summand above with index
(i,j)
and discuss three cases.

Case 1:
i<j
.

We first re-write
V^{\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j}}-V^{\bar{x}_{i+1},\bar{y}_{j}}
. Notice that the value difference between the policy pairs
(\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j})
and
(\bar{x}_{i+1},\bar{y}_{j})
starts at step
i
, since both policy pairs are equal to
(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})
from step
0
to step
i-1
. At the
i
th step,
\bar{x}_{i}
changes to
x
while
\bar{x}_{i+1}
remains as
x^{\prime}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle V^{\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j}}\,-\,V^{\bar{x}_{i+1},\bar{y}_{j}}

\displaystyle=\;\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\sum_{s,a,b}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s;i)x(a\,|\,s)y^{\prime}(b\,|\,s)\left(r(s,a,b)+\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t\,=\,i+1}^{\infty}\gamma^{t-i}r(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})~\bigg|~s_{i+1}\sim p(\cdot\,|\,s,a,b),\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\sum_{s,a,b}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s;i)x^{\prime}(a\,|\,s)y^{\prime}(b\,|\,s)\left(r(s,a,b)+\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t\,=\,i+1}^{\infty}\gamma^{t-i}r(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})~\bigg|~s_{i+1}\sim p(\cdot\,|\,s,a,b),\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\;\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\sum_{s,a,b}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s;i)\Big(x(a\,|\,s)-x^{\prime}(a\,|\,s)\Big)y^{\prime}(b\,|\,s)\Bigg(r(s,a,b)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\,\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t\,=\,i+1}^{\infty}\gamma^{t-i}r(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})~\bigg|~s_{i+1}\sim p(\cdot\,|\,s,a,b),\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j}\right]\Bigg)

where we define

\displaystyle d_{\mu}^{x,y}(s;i)\;=\;(1-\gamma)\mathbb{E}\left[\gamma^{i}\boldsymbol{1}[s_{i}=s]~|~s_{0}\sim\mu\right].

(note that
d_{\mu}^{x,y}(s)=\sum_{i\,=\,0}^{\infty}d_{\mu}^{x,y}(s;i)
).

Similarly,

\displaystyle V^{\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j+1}}\,-\,V^{\bar{x}_{i+1},\bar{y}_{j+1}}

\displaystyle=\;\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\sum_{s,a,b}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s;i)\Big(x(a\,|\,s)-x^{\prime}(a\,|\,s)\Big)y^{\prime}(b|s)\Bigg(r(s,a,b)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\,\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t\,=\,i+1}^{\infty}\gamma^{t-i}r(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})~\bigg|~s_{i+1}\sim p(\cdot\,|\,s,a,b),\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j+1}\right]\Bigg).

We notice that the following difference:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t\,=\,i+1}^{\infty}\gamma^{t-i}r(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})~\bigg|~s_{i+1}\sim p(\cdot\,|\,s,a,b),\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j}\right]\,-\,\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t\,=\,i+1}^{\infty}\gamma^{t-i}r(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})~\bigg|~s_{i+1}\sim p(\cdot\,|\,s,a,b),\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j+1}\right]

is equivalent to

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\sum_{\widetilde{s},\widetilde{a},\widetilde{b}}d_{p(\cdot\,|\,s,a,b)}^{x,y^{\prime}}(\widetilde{s};j-i-1)x(\widetilde{a}\,|\,\widetilde{s})\Big(y(\widetilde{b}\,|\,\widetilde{s})-y^{\prime}(\widetilde{b}\,|\,\widetilde{s})\Big)Q^{x,y}(\widetilde{s},\widetilde{a},\widetilde{b}).

Hence,

\displaystyle V^{\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j}}-V^{\bar{x}_{i+1},\bar{y}_{j}}-V^{\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j+1}}+V^{\bar{x}_{i+1},\bar{y}_{j+1}}

\displaystyle=\;\frac{\gamma}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\sum_{s,a,b}\sum_{\widetilde{s},\widetilde{a},\widetilde{b}}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s;i)d_{p(\cdot\,|\,s,a,b)}^{x,y^{\prime}}(\widetilde{s};j-i-1)\Big(x(a\,|\,s)-x^{\prime}(a\,|\,s)\Big)y^{\prime}(b\,|\,s)x(\widetilde{a}\,|\,\widetilde{s})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\,\Big(y(\widetilde{b}\,|\,\widetilde{s})-y^{\prime}(\widetilde{b}\,|\,\widetilde{s})\Big)Q^{x,y}(\widetilde{s},\widetilde{a},\widetilde{b})

\displaystyle\leq\;\frac{\gamma}{2(1-\gamma)^{3}}\sum_{s,a,b}\sum_{\widetilde{s},\widetilde{a},\widetilde{b}}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s;i)d_{p(\cdot\,|\,s,a,b)}^{x,y^{\prime}}(\widetilde{s};j-i-1)y^{\prime}(b\,|\,s)x(\widetilde{a}\,|\,\widetilde{s})\Big(x(a\,|\,s)-x^{\prime}(a\,|\,s)\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\,\frac{\gamma}{2(1-\gamma)^{3}}\sum_{s,a,b}\sum_{\widetilde{s},\widetilde{a},\widetilde{b}}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s;i)d_{p(\cdot\,|\,s,a,b)}^{x,y^{\prime}}(\widetilde{s};j-i-1)y^{\prime}(b\,|\,s)x(\widetilde{a}\,|\,\widetilde{s})\Big(y(\widetilde{b}\,|\,\widetilde{s})-y^{\prime}(\widetilde{b}\,|\,\widetilde{s})\Big)^{2}

(bounding
|Q^{x,y}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)|
by
\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
and using AM-GM)

\displaystyle=\;\frac{\gamma A}{2(1-\gamma)^{3}}\sum_{s,a}\sum_{\widetilde{s}}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s;i)d_{p(\cdot\,|\,s,a,y^{\prime})}^{x,y^{\prime}}(\widetilde{s};j-i-1)\Big(x(a\,|\,s)-x^{\prime}(a\,|\,s)\Big)^{2}

(define
p(\cdot\,|\,s,a,y)=\sum_{b}p(\cdot\,|\,s,a,b)y(b|s)
)

\displaystyle\qquad+\,\frac{\gamma A}{2(1-\gamma)^{3}}\sum_{s}\sum_{\widetilde{s},\widetilde{b}}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s;i)d_{p(\cdot\,|\,s,\text{unif},y^{\prime})}^{x,y^{\prime}}(\widetilde{s};j-i-1)\Big(y(\widetilde{b}\,|\,\widetilde{s})-y^{\prime}(\widetilde{b}\,|\,\widetilde{s})\Big)^{2}.

(define uniform distribution
\text{unif}=\frac{1}{A}\boldsymbol{1}
)

Summing the inequality above over
i<j
yields

\displaystyle\sum_{i\,=\,0}^{\infty}\sum_{j\,=\,i+1}^{\infty}\left(V^{\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j}}-V^{\bar{x}_{i+1},\bar{y}_{j}}-V^{\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j+1}}+V^{\bar{x}_{i+1},\bar{y}_{j+1}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\;\frac{\gamma A}{2(1-\gamma)^{3}}\sum_{i\,=\,0}^{\infty}\sum_{s,a}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s;i)\Big(x(a\,|\,s)-x^{\prime}(a\,|\,s)\Big)^{2}\left(\sum_{\widetilde{s}}\sum_{j\,=\,i+1}^{\infty}d^{x,y^{\prime}}_{p(\cdot\,|\,s,a,y^{\prime})}(\widetilde{s};j-i-1)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\,\frac{\gamma A}{2(1-\gamma)^{3}}\sum_{i\,=\,0}^{\infty}\sum_{\widetilde{s},\widetilde{b}}\sum_{s}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s;i)\Big(y(\widetilde{b}\,|\,\widetilde{s})-y^{\prime}(\widetilde{b}\,|\,\widetilde{s})\Big)^{2}\left(\sum_{j\,=\,i+1}^{\infty}d^{x,y^{\prime}}_{p(\cdot\,|\,s,\text{unif},y^{\prime})}(\widetilde{s};j-i-1)\right)

\displaystyle=\;\frac{\gamma A}{2(1-\gamma)^{3}}\sum_{s,a}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s)\Big(x(a\,|\,s)-x^{\prime}(a\,|\,s)\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\,\frac{\gamma A}{2(1-\gamma)^{3}}\sum_{\widetilde{s},\widetilde{b}}\sum_{s}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s)d^{x,y^{\prime}}_{p(\cdot\,|\,s,\text{unif},y^{\prime})}(\widetilde{s})\Big(y(\widetilde{b}\,|\,\widetilde{s})-y^{\prime}(\widetilde{b}\,|\,\widetilde{s})\Big)^{2}

(using the property:
\sum_{i\,=\,0}^{\infty}d_{\mu}^{x,y}(s;i)=d_{\mu}^{x,y}(s)
)

\displaystyle=\;\frac{\gamma A}{2(1-\gamma)^{3}}\sum_{s}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s)\left\|{x(\cdot\,|\,s)-x^{\prime}(\cdot\,|\,s)}\right\|^{2}+\frac{\gamma A}{2(1-\gamma)^{3}}\sum_{s}d^{x,y^{\prime}}_{\mu^{\prime}}(s)\left\|{y(\cdot\,|\,s)-y^{\prime}(\cdot\,|\,s)}\right\|^{2}

where
\mu^{\prime}
is a state distribution that generates the state by the following procedure: first sample a state
s_{0}
according to
d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(\cdot)
, then execute
(\text{unif},y^{\prime})=(\frac{1}{A}\boldsymbol{1},y^{\prime})
for one step, and then output the next state.

By
Lemma
22
(with
\pi=(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})
,
\pi^{\prime}=(x,y^{\prime})
, and
\bar{\pi}=(\text{unif},y^{\prime})
), we have
\frac{d_{\mu^{\prime}}^{x,y^{\prime}}(s)}{d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s)}\leq\frac{d_{\mu^{\prime}}^{x,y^{\prime}}(s)}{\mu(1-\gamma)}\leq\frac{\kappa_{\mu}^{2}}{\gamma(1-\gamma)}
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{i\,=\,0}^{\infty}\sum_{j\,=\,i+1}^{\infty}\left(V^{\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j}}-V^{\bar{x}_{i+1},\bar{y}_{j}}-V^{\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{j+1}}+V^{\bar{x}_{i+1},\bar{y}_{j+1}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\;\frac{\kappa_{\mu}^{2}A}{2(1-\gamma)^{4}}\sum_{s}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s)\left(\left\|{x(\cdot\,|\,s)-x^{\prime}(\cdot\,|\,s)}\right\|^{2}+\left\|{y(\cdot\,|\,s)-y^{\prime}(\cdot\,|\,s)}\right\|^{2}\right).

Case 2:
i>j
.

This case is symmetric to the case of
i<j
, and can be handled similarly.

Case 3:
i=j
.

In this case,

\displaystyle\sum_{i\,=\,0}^{\infty}\left(V^{\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{i}}-V^{\bar{x}_{i+1},\bar{y}_{i}}-V^{\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{i+1}}+V^{\bar{x}_{i+1},\bar{y}_{i+1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\;\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\sum_{i\,=\,0}^{\infty}\sum_{s,a,b}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s;i)\Big(x^{\prime}(a\,|\,s)-x(a\,|\,s)\Big)\Big(y^{\prime}(a\,|\,s)-y(a\,|\,s)\Big)Q^{x,y}(s,a,b)

\displaystyle=\;\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\sum_{s,a,b}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s)\Big(x^{\prime}(a|s)-x(a|s)\Big)\Big(y^{\prime}(a|s)-y(a|s)\Big)Q^{x,y}(s,a,b)

\displaystyle\leq\;\frac{1}{2(1-\gamma)^{2}}\sum_{s,a,b}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s)\Big(x^{\prime}(a\,|\,s)-x(a\,|\,s)\Big)^{2}+\frac{1}{2(1-\gamma)^{2}}\sum_{s,a,b}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s)\Big(y^{\prime}(a\,|\,s)-y(a\,|\,s)\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle=\;\frac{A}{2(1-\gamma)^{2}}\sum_{s}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s)\left\|{x^{\prime}(\cdot\,|\,s)-x(\cdot\,|\,s)}\right\|^{2}+\frac{A}{2(1-\gamma)^{2}}\sum_{s}d_{\mu}^{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(s)\left\|{y^{\prime}(\cdot\,|\,s)-y(\cdot\,|\,s)}\right\|^{2}.

Summing the bounds in all three cases above completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 22
.

Let
\pi
,
\pi^{\prime}
and
\bar{\pi}
be three policies, and
\mu
be some initial distribution. Let
\mu^{\prime}
be a state distribution that generates a state according to the following: first sample an
s_{0}
from
d_{\mu}^{\pi}(\cdot)
, then execute
\bar{\pi}
for one step, and then output the next state. Then,

\displaystyle\left\|{\frac{d^{\pi^{\prime}}_{\mu^{\prime}}}{\mu}}\right\|_{\infty}\;\leq\;\frac{\kappa_{\mu}^{2}}{\gamma}\;\triangleq\;\frac{1}{\gamma}\left(\sup_{\widetilde{\pi}}\left\|{\frac{d^{\widetilde{\pi}}_{\mu}}{\mu}}\right\|_{\infty}\right)^{2}.

Proof of
Lemma
22
.

For a particular state
s^{\sharp}
, we view the supremum
\sup_{\widetilde{\pi}}\frac{d_{\mu}^{\widetilde{\pi}}(s^{\sharp})}{\mu(s^{\sharp})}
as the optimal value of an MDP whose reward function is
r(s,a)=\frac{1-\gamma}{\mu(s^{\sharp})}\boldsymbol{1}[s=s^{\sharp}]
and initial state is generated by
\mu
. The optimal value of this MDP is upper bounded by
\kappa_{\mu}
by
Definition
1
. We next consider the following non-stationary policy for this MDP: first execute
\bar{\pi}
for one step, and then execute
\pi^{\prime}
in the rest of the steps. The discounted value of this non-stationary policy is lower bounded by

\displaystyle\gamma\sum_{s}\Pr\left(s_{1}=s~|~s_{0}\sim\mu,\,a_{0}\sim\bar{\pi}(\cdot\,|\,s_{0})\right)\times\frac{d_{s}^{\pi^{\prime}}(s^{\sharp})}{\mu(s^{\sharp})}\;=\;\gamma\sum_{s_{0},a_{0},s}\mu(s_{0})\bar{\pi}(a_{0}|s_{0})p(s~|s_{0},a_{0})\times\frac{d_{s}^{\pi^{\prime}}(s^{\sharp})}{\mu(s^{\sharp})}.

We can upper and lower bound the discounted sum above as the following:

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{\kappa_{\mu}}\sum_{s_{0},a_{0},s}d_{\mu}^{\pi}(s_{0})\bar{\pi}(a_{0}|s_{0})p(s~|s_{0},a_{0})\times\frac{d_{s}^{\pi^{\prime}}(s^{\sharp})}{\mu(s^{\sharp})}\;\leq\;\gamma\sum_{s_{0},a_{0},s}\mu(s_{0})\bar{\pi}(a_{0}|s_{0})p(s~|s_{0},a_{0})\times\frac{d_{s}^{\pi^{\prime}}(s^{\sharp})}{\mu(s^{\sharp})}\;\leq\;\kappa_{\mu}.

where the right inequality is due to that this discounted value must be upper bounded by the optimal value of this MDP, which has an upper bound
\kappa_{\mu}
, and the left inequality is by the definition of
\kappa_{\mu}
. Now notice that

\displaystyle\mu^{\prime}(s)\;=\;\sum_{s_{0},a_{0}}d^{\pi}_{\mu}(s_{0})\bar{\pi}(a_{0}|s_{0})p(s|s_{0},a_{0})

by the definition of
\mu^{\prime}
. Plugging this into the previous inequality, we get

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{\kappa_{\mu}}\times\frac{d_{\mu^{\prime}}^{\pi^{\prime}}(s^{\sharp})}{\mu(s^{\sharp})}\;\leq\;\kappa_{\mu}.

Since this holds for any
s^{\sharp}
, this gives

\displaystyle\left\|{\frac{d^{\pi^{\prime}}_{\mu^{\prime}}}{\mu}}\right\|_{\infty}\;\leq\;\frac{\kappa_{\mu}^{2}}{\gamma}.

∎

Appendix F
Auxiliary Lemmas for Stochastic Projected Gradient Descent

Algorithm
2
serves a sample-based algorithm if we solve the empirical risk minimization problem
(
7
)
via a stochastic projected gradient descent,

{w}_{i}^{(k+1)}\;=\;\mathcal{P}_{\left\|{w}\right\|\,\leq\,W}\left({w}_{i}^{(k)}\,-\,\lambda^{(k)}\,\widehat{\nabla}_{i}^{(t)}(s^{(k)},a_{i}^{(k)}))\right)

(38)

where
\widehat{\nabla}_{i}^{(t)}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=2(\langle\phi_{i},w_{i}^{(k)}\rangle-R_{i}^{(k)})\phi_{i}
is the
k
th gradient of
(
7
)
and
\lambda^{(k)}>0
is the stepsize. We assume that the smallest eigenvalue of correlation matrix
\mathbb{E}_{s,a_{i}}\left[\phi_{i}(s,a_{i})\phi_{i}(s,a_{i})^{\top}\right]
is positive.

For a constrained convex optimization,
\minimize_{w\,\in\,\{w\,|\,\left\|{w}\right\|\,\leq\,W\}}f(w)
, where
f(w)
is a convex function and
W>0
, we consider a basic method for solving this problem: the stochastic projected gradient descent in
Algorithm
4
, where
\mathcal{P}_{\left\|{w}\right\|\,\leq\,W}
is a Euclidean projection in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to the constraint set
\left\|{w}\right\|\leq W
.

Algorithm 4

Stochastic projected gradient descent with weighted averaging

1:

Parameters:

W
,
\lambda^{(k)}
, and
\beta_{k}^{(K)}
.

2:

Input
: Stepsize
\alpha
, total number of iterations
K>0
.

3:

Initialization
:
w^{(0)}=0
.

4:

for
step
k=1,\ldots,K

do

5:

Draw
\nabla^{(k)}
form a distribution such that
\mathbb{E}[\nabla^{(k)}\,|\,w^{(k)}]\in\partial f(w^{(k)})
.

6:

Update
w^{(k+1)}=\mathcal{P}_{\left\|{w}\right\|\,\leq\,W}\left(w^{(k)}-\lambda^{(k)}\nabla^{(k)}\right)
.

7:

end

for

8:

Output
:
\sum_{k\,=\,0}^{K}\beta_{k}^{(K)}w^{(k)}
.

Lemma 23
.

Let
w^{\star}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\argmin_{w\,\in\,\{w\,|\,\left\|{w}\right\|\,\leq\,W\}}f(w)
. Suppose
\text{Var}(\nabla^{(k)})\leq\sigma^{2}
. If we run
Algorithm
4
with stepsize
\lambda^{(k)}=O(\frac{1}{1+k})
and
\beta_{k}^{(K)}=\frac{1/\lambda^{(k)}}{\sum_{r\,=\,0}^{K}1/\lambda^{(r)}}
, then,

\mathbb{E}\left[f\left(\sum_{k\,=\,0}^{K}\beta_{k}^{(K)}w^{(k)}\right)\right]-f(w^{\star})\;\lesssim\;\frac{\sigma^{2}W^{2}d}{K}.

Proof of
Lemma
23
.

See the proof of Theorem 1 in
(
Cohen et al. 2017b
)
.
∎

Appendix G
Additional Experiments

We provide details about our experiments as follows.

For illustration, we consider the state space
{\mathcal{S}}=\{\textit{safe},\textit{distancing}\}
and action space
{\mathcal{A}}_{i}=\{A,B,C,D\}
, and the number of players
N=8
. In each state
s\in{\mathcal{S}}
, the reward for player
i
taking an action
a\in{\mathcal{A}}_{i}
is the
w_{s}^{a}
-weighted number of players using the action
a
, where
w_{s}^{a}
specifies the action preference
w_{s}^{A}<w_{s}^{B}<w_{s}^{C}<w_{s}^{D}
. The reward in state
distancing
is less than that in state
safe
by a large amount
c>0
. For state transition, if more than half of players find themselves using the same action, then the state transits to the state
distancing
; transition back to the state
safe
whenever no more than half of players take the same action.

In our experiments, we implement our independent policy gradient method based on the code for the projected stochastic gradient ascent
(
Leonardos et al. 2022
)
. At each iteration, we collect a batch of
20
trajectories to estimate the action-value function and (or) the stationary state distribution under current policy. We choose the discount factor
\gamma=0.99
, and different the stepsize
\eta
, and initial state distributions as we report next.

Continuing
Section
7
, we further report our computational results using stepsize
\eta=0.001
in
Figure
2
, larger stepsize
\eta=0.002
in
Figure
3
and stepsize
\eta=0.005
in
Figure
4
. We notice that the stepsize
\eta=0.001
for the projected stochastic gradient ascent
(
Leonardos et al. 2022
)
does not yield convergence while our independent policy gradient converges as shown in
Figure
2
. As demonstrated in
Section
7
, our independent policy gradient permits larger stepsizes with fast convergence, e.g.,
\eta=0.002
in
Figure
3
and
\eta=0.005
in
Figure
4
. Compared
Figure
3
with
Figure
4
, we see an improved convergence of our independent policy gradient using a larger stepsize. We also remark that the learnt policies for all these experiments can generate the same Nash policy that matches the result in
Leonardos et al. 2022
.

. In our computational experiments, the initial distribution
\rho
is uniform.

(b) Learning curves for six individual runs of our independent policy gradient (solid line) and the projected stochastic gradient ascent (dash line) three each.
(c) Distribution of players in one of two states taking four actions.
In (a) and (b), we measure the accuracy by the absolute distance of each iterate to the converged Nash policy, i.e.,
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\,=\,1}^{N}\|{\pi_{i}^{(t)}-\pi_{i}^{\text{Nash}}}\|_{1}
. In our computational experiments, the initial distribution
\rho
is uniform.

Convergence performance.

In (a) and (b), we measure the accuracy by the absolute distance of each iterate to the converged Nash policy, i.e.,
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\,=\,1}^{N}\|{\pi_{i}^{(t)}-\pi_{i}^{\text{Nash}}}\|_{1}
. In our computational experiments, the initial distribution is nearly degenerate
\rho=(0.0001,0.9999)
.

We also examine how sensitive the performance of algorithms depends on initial state distributions. As discussed in
Section
4
, our independent policy gradient method
(
4
)
is different from the projected policy gradient
(
4.1
)
by removing the dependence on the initial state distribution. In the policy gradient theory
(
Agarwal et al. 2021
)
, convergence of projected policy gradient methods is often restricted by how explorative the initial state distribution is. To be fair, we choose stepsize
\eta=0.001
for our algorithm since it achieves a similar performance as the projected stochastic gradient ascent
(
Leonardos et al. 2022
)
in
Figure
2
. We choose two different initial state distributions
\rho=(0.9999,0.0001)
and
\rho=(0.0001,0.9999)
and report our computational results in
Figure
5
and
Figure
6
, respectively. Compared
Figure
5
with
Figure
2
, both algorithms become a bit slower, but our algorithm is relatively insusceptible to the change of
\rho
. This becomes more clearer in
Figure
6
for another
\rho=(0.0001,0.9999)
. This demonstrates that practical performance of our independent policy gradient method
(
4
)
indeed is invariant to the initial distribution
\rho
.
